    "newString": "Our contribution is the phase-diagram methodology, not frontier-scale applicability ($>$7B untested); triggered cessation on entropy reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:intervention}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Granger precedence is not causation.}\nOur claim is scoped to temporal predictive improvement~\\cite{granger1969investigating,shojaie2022granger}; bivariate tests cannot exclude confounders, and our cluster-adjusted FPR CI ($[0\\%, 41\\%]$; \\S\\ref{sec:limitations}) means true Type~I error may be non-negligible.\n\n\\paragraph{Non-confirming cells.}\nFive $\\alpha{=}0.60$ cells are excluded from the boundary fit (sync-dip zone: 0/8 at $T{\\leq}20$, dissolving to 5/8 at $T{=}25$); the remaining 15/15 match theory (LOO-CV MAE${=}0.037$).\nThe systematic 2/8 gap reflects MAUVE floor saturation ($\\mu{=}0.029$).\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-architecture failure modes.}\nFive of fifteen conditions do not confirm forward detection (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}), spanning GPT-2 precision/confounding, Pythia-1.4B seed dependence, and SmolLM3-3B entropy decoupling (whose non-monotonic entropy violates Proposition~\\ref{thm:entropy_leads}'s monotonic-decline assumption, correctly predicting failure).\nPhase-diagram structure is qualitatively preserved at 1.4B ($\\alpha_c$ onset consistent, sync-dip absent, forward detection seed-robust at $T{=}10$); directional asymmetry is seed-sensitive above onset (s43 reverse 6/6 at $\\alpha{\\geq}0.60$; s42: 0/6; Appendix~\\ref{app:scale_14b}).\n\n\\paragraph{Implications.}\nThe ${\\geq}1$-generation entropy-diversity lag (14/15 experiments; Proposition~\\ref{prop:div_lag}) and PermG directional asymmetry (6/8 forward, 0/8 reverse) support a structural canary mechanism, though directionality is domain-dependent (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur contribution is the phase-diagram methodology, not frontier-scale applicability ($>$7B untested); PermG requires fp32; triggered cessation on entropy reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:intervention}).\n",

   - **Reviewer #184 claim audit**: 3 fixes — naive baseline main text reference, T range update, SmolLM3 Proposition 1 explanation
   - Throughout: maintain EMNLP 8-page main text limit

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - EMNLP 2026 ARR: 8-page main text limit; Limitations section counts toward main text; appendix unlimited
   - 6-pair basis: 2 canaries (token_entropy, ECE) × 3 downstreams (distinct_1/2/3); MAUVE excluded at 1.4B due to floor ~0.033
   - 8-pair basis: 2 canaries × 4 downstreams (incl. MAUVE); used for 410M and cross-architecture Table 2
   - PermG (Permutation Granger causality) with BH-FDR at q=0.05
   - Phase diagram: α × T grid, detection boundary α_c(T) = A·T^{-γ}
   - Naive baselines: PPL-slope (≥2 consecutive rises) and Variance-threshold (2σ) vs PermG
   - K≥3 consensus: multi-method agreement threshold; degrades to single-method test at T=10
   - LaTeX appendix section counter limit: max 26 sections (A-Z)
   - 64% perplexity reduction (s42 single seed: 1 - 56.99/157.09)

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **docs/paper/appendix.tex** — Most heavily modified file
     - **Table 17** (L495-516 area): Simplified from 5-column to 4-column all-/6 basis
       ```latex
       \begin{tabular}{lcccc}
       \toprule

                & s42 & s44 & s42 & s43 \\

       ```latex
       On the 6-pair basis (MAUVE excluded due to systematic floor ${\sim}0.033$; Table~\ref{tab:14b_phase}), PermG forward detection at 1.4B ranges from 0/6 ($\alpha{=}0.45$, $T{=}10$) to 6/6 ($\alpha{\geq}0.60$, $T{=}15$); token entropy exhibits one-step saturation ($27$--$39\%$ drop from generation~0 to~1), reducing temporal resolution for entropy-based pairs.
       Seed s42 shows PermG$_\text{rev}{=}0/6$ across all eight 1.4B conditions ($4\alpha{\times}2T$), confirming that bidirectionality is seed-specific rather than scale-inherent.
       The 410M sync-dip ($\alpha{=}0.60$: 0/8 at $T{\leq}20$) does not reproduce at 1.4B (4/6 at $T{=}10$)...
       ```

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "9951e3ac-3b73-4d67-84c0-521627de6c05",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Granger precedence is not causation.}\nOur claim is scoped to temporal predictive improvement~\\cite{granger1969investigating,shojaie2022granger}; bivariate tests cannot exclude confounders, and our cluster-adjusted FPR CI ($[0\\%, 41\\%]$; \\S\\ref{sec:limitations}) means true Type~I error may be non-negligible.\n\n\\paragraph{Non-confirming cells.}\nFive $\\alpha{=}0.60$ cells are excluded from the boundary fit (sync-dip zone: 0/8 at $T{\\leq}20$, dissolving to 5/8 at $T{=}25$); the remaining 15/15 match theory (LOO-CV MAE${=}0.037$).\nThe systematic 2/8 gap reflects MAUVE floor saturation ($\\mu{=}0.029$).\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-architecture failure modes.}\nFive of fifteen conditions do not confirm forward detection (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}), spanning GPT-2 precision/confounding, Pythia-1.4B seed dependence, and SmolLM3-3B entropy decoupling (whose non-monotonic entropy violates Proposition~\\ref{thm:entropy_leads}'s monotonic-decline assumption, correctly predicting failure).\nPhase-diagram structure is qualitatively preserved at 1.4B ($\\alpha_c$ onset consistent, sync-dip absent, forward detection seed-robust at $T{=}10$); directional asymmetry is seed-sensitive above onset (s43 reverse 6/6 at $\\alpha{\\geq}0.60$; s42: 0/6; Appendix~\\ref{app:scale_14b}).\n\n\\paragraph{Implications.}\nThe ${\\geq}1$-generation entropy-diversity lag (14/15 experiments; Proposition~\\ref{prop:div_lag}) and PermG directional asymmetry (6/8 forward, 0/8 reverse) support a structural canary mechanism, though directionality is domain-dependent (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\nOur contribution is the phase-diagram methodology, not frontier-scale applicability ($>$7B untested); triggered cessation on entropy reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:intervention}).\n",
        "numLines": 17,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 17
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/discussion.tex"
  },
  "type": "attachment",

_source line 12143 · 2026-05-24T03:31:47.451Z_

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "2078ee21-bd6b-40b5-838f-41ac96d6f761",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/docs/paper/method.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/docs/paper/method.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\paragraph{Design considerations.}\n\\label{sec:pitfalls}\nLead-lag analysis in iterative model collapse is fragile along three axes, each documented with data from \\S\\ref{sec:regime}--\\ref{sec:dose_response}.\n\\emph{Method sensitivity}: five detection methods applied to identical data yield forward significance rates spanning 0\\% to 92\\%, with inter-method agreement at $\\kappa{=}0.185$.\n\\emph{Spurious regression}: first-differencing eliminates canary$\\to$perplexity associations ($F{\\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$) while preserving canary$\\to$diversity (24/24$\\to$24/24), exposing an entire analysis channel as trend-driven~\\cite{granger1974spurious}.\n\\emph{Regime dependence}: the same Granger test shifts from 0/8 to 6/8 significant pairs within a 2-percentage-point interval ($\\alpha{=}0.48 \\to 0.50$).\n\n\\subsection{Method Sensitivity Analysis}\n\\label{sec:mmct}\nWe compare five lead-lag detection methods---naive cross-correlation, differenced cross-correlation, threshold onset, permutation Granger causality (PermG), and Toda--Yamamoto~\\cite{toda1995statistical}---in a multi-method comparison~\\cite{simonsohn2020specification} calibrated against null-control ($\\alpha{=}0.0$) data where any detection is a false positive.\nDetection results use equal-weight majority voting ($K{\\geq}3$ of 5 methods, each contributing one vote per pair) with forward-direction filtering; FPR-inverse weighting is used only in exploratory method ranking (Appendix~\\ref{app:weight_robustness}).\nEach method embodies different assumptions: naive cross-correlation is assumption-free but produces 92\\% null-control FPR; differenced cross-correlation controls for trends but lacks directional separation; threshold onset is power-limited at $T{=}11$; Toda--Yamamoto avoids pre-testing for unit roots; PermG uses a nonparametric permutation null with built-in directionality.\nPooled null-control FPR across three seeds (cluster-adjusted 95\\% CI): PermG 0\\% $[0, 41]$, Toda--Yamamoto 17\\% $[0, 25]$, DiffXCorr 38\\% $[25, 50]$, NaiveXCorr 92\\% $[88, 100]$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:fpr_independence}).\nDiffXCorr's empirical FPR (38\\%) substantially exceeds its simulated rate (4.2\\% under AR(1) DGP), indicating that model-collapse nonstationarity extends beyond simple autoregressive structure.\nUnder all four weighting schemes (equal, FPR-inverse, capped, rank-based), PermG is the only method maintaining zero null-control FPR alongside consistent detection at $\\alpha \\geq 0.50$.\nPractitioner-facing alternatives (PPL-slope, variance thresholds) yield 67--100\\% FPR under natural training drift (Appendix~\\ref{app:naive_baselines}).\nThis finding---that method choice alone can shift conclusions from universal significance to complete null on identical data---motivates reporting method sensitivity as a primary result rather than relegating it to a robustness check.\n\n\\paragraph{Permutation Granger causality.}\nPermG tests whether past values of canary metric $X$ improve prediction of downstream metric $Y$ beyond $Y$'s own history; Granger precedence establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causality~\\cite{shojaie2022granger}.\nWe use classical rather than neural Granger methods~\\citep{tank2021neural} because $T{\\leq}25$ provides insufficient observations for neural network parameter estimation.\nWe apply 5{,}000 permutations~\\citep{phipson2010permutation} on first-differenced series with Benjamini--Hochberg FDR correction at $q{=}0.05$ across 8 canary$\\to$downstream pairs (2 canaries $\\times$ 4 downstream metrics, sharing canary terms and thus positively dependent).\nThe permutation test controls Type~I error exactly by construction (exchangeable null distribution); BH-FDR remains valid under positive regression dependence on a subset (PRDS)~\\cite{benjamini2001control}, and 0/24 null-control rejections across three seeds empirically confirm adequate size control (Clopper--Pearson 97.5\\% upper bound: 14\\%; within-seed ICC${=}0.34$, cluster-adjusted 95\\% CI $[0\\%, 41\\%]$; Appendix~\\ref{app:fpr_independence}).\nSimulation under non-stationary AR(1) DGP ($\\varphi{=}0.95$--$0.99$) reveals PermG FPR inflation to ${\\sim}10\\%$; however, our empirical series exhibit low stochastic persistence (null token entropy $\\varphi{=}-0.51$; first-differenced distinct-1 $\\varphi{<}0.44$), and 0/24 null rejections confirm adequate size control (Appendix~\\ref{app:nonstationarity}).\nThe same simulation shows PermG retains ${\\sim}80\\%$ of raw-levels power at $T{=}11$ versus ${\\sim}61\\%$ for standard first-differencing, justifying the permutation approach at short series lengths.\n\n\\subsection{Detection Boundary}\n\\label{sec:theory}\n\nWe model the PermG detection rate as a $T$-dependent steep sigmoid, excluding the non-monotonic dip at $\\alpha \\in [0.55, 0.65]$:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\mathrm{rate}(\\alpha, T) = \\frac{0.70}{1 + \\exp\\!\\bigl(-100\\,(\\alpha - \\alpha_c(T))\\bigr)},\n\\label{eq:joint-sigmoid}\n\\end{equation}\nfitted to $T{=}10$ data ($n{=}11$, sync-dip excluded; $R^2{=}0.969$).\nThe detection boundary follows a power law in observation length:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\alpha_c(T) = A \\cdot T^{-\\gamma},\n\\label{eq:alpha-c}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere the exponent $\\gamma$ is determined by the sub-linear signal model below.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Empirical detection boundary]\\label{prop:phase-boundary}\nUnder an empirical sub-linear signal model $S(\\alpha) = c \\cdot \\alpha^\\beta$ with $\\beta < 1$, the detection boundary satisfies\n\\begin{equation}\n\\alpha_c(T) = A \\cdot T^{-1/(2\\beta)},\n\\label{eq:alpha-c-theory}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $A = (z^* \\sigma / c)^{1/\\beta}$ and $z^* = \\Phi^{-1}(\\pi^*)$ \\textup{(proof in Appendix~\\ref{app:alpha_c_proof})}.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\noindent The signal model $S(\\alpha) = c\\alpha^\\beta$ is an empirical parametrization: $\\beta$ is fitted from data rather than derived from training dynamics.\nThe power-law form $\\alpha_c \\propto T^{-1/(2\\beta)}$ follows from signal-detection theory (signal-to-noise crossing a threshold), but $\\beta$ requires architecture-specific calibration.\n\nCross-$T$ validation (train $T{\\in}\\{10,20\\}$, test $T{\\in}\\{5,15\\}$) yields $\\beta{=}0.24$, $\\gamma{=}2.08$ (MAE${=}0.106$, CI $6/6$); leave-one-$T$-out CV on the full grid ($T{\\in}\\{5,8,10,15,20\\}$, sync-dip excluded) confirms predictive accuracy (MAE${=}0.037$, CI $15/15$; Appendix~\\ref{app:alpha_c_proof}).\nHowever, $\\beta$ is poorly identified: MLE $\\hat{\\beta}{=}0.66$ with profile 95\\% CI $[0.24, 0.78]$, and model comparison favors the power-probit (M2) over a linear boundary (M1) only when sync-dip data are included ($\\Delta\\mathrm{AIC}{=}4.15$ without; Appendix~\\ref{app:alpha_c_proof}).\n\n\\paragraph{Shape comparison with smooth alternatives.}\nA concave signal model ($\\beta'{=}0.5$) produces $\\gamma'{=}1.87$, close to the observed $2.08$, so the scaling exponent alone cannot distinguish smooth from steep-sigmoid boundaries.\nFormal shape testing on dense $T{=}10$ data (17~$\\alpha$ values) favors a smooth sigmoid over a literal step function ($\\Delta\\mathrm{AIC}{=}-110$), but the fitted onset steepness ($k{=}15.7$, 95\\% CI $[9.4, 28.2]$; \\S\\ref{sec:dose_response}) is $3$--$4{\\times}$ higher than any smooth signal model predicts.\nVAR(1) power simulations (1{,}000 per cell; Appendix~\\ref{app:power_sim}) under H0 (linear) and H0$'$ (concave) produce onset sigmoids with $k{\\approx}5$--$6$, yielding $k_{\\mathrm{obs}} / k_{\\mathrm{H0}} \\approx 3$ (CI lower bound $k{=}9.4$, ratio ${\\approx}2$).\nThis steepness gap indicates a significantly sharper transition than statistical power alone can produce, consistent with a genuine but finite-width phase boundary rather than a gradual power artifact.\nSince $\\alpha_\\infty{\\approx}0$, any nonzero contamination produces signal ($S \\propto \\alpha^{0.24}$), but sub-linear growth makes detection a function of $T$.\n",
        "numLines": 67,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 67
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/method.tex"
  },

Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. 1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Our landscape characterization uses Pythia-410M as the primary architecture; the phase diagram is Pythia-family-only (410M and 1.4B), and frontier-scale behavior ($>$7B) is untested.
5	With $T{\leq}25$ observations, statistical power remains limited ($\hat{\beta}{=}0.66$, CI $[0.24, 0.78]$); PermG's empirical FPR of $4/36$ (11.1\%, 6 seeds) is the lowest among directionally-specific methods but non-zero; the cluster-adjusted 95\% CI is $[1.2\%, 30.4\%]$ (within-seed ICC${=}0.14$).
6	The sync-dip mechanism at $\alpha{=}0.60$ remains open, and all experiments use a single generation strategy (temperature~$1.0$, top-$k{=}50$); alternative decoding settings may alter the collapse dynamics.
7

13	# Model Collapse — Writing Skill (8 deep-read papers)
14	
15	## 叙事原型
16	
17	**主流: "破局型"** (5/8) — "先前定性 X 有误/不够，我们发现机制 Z"。He2025: "方差太慢→熵"; Dohmatob2025: "mixing 应管用→不管用"; Noci2024: "已知 depth collapse→发现 width collapse"。**次主流: "统一框架型"** (2/8) — 解释矛盾结论属不同 regime。Kazdan2025, Dohmatob2024。高。
18	
19	## Hook 策略
20	
21	**"数据危机" + 具体数字** (5/8): "17T tokens" (Guo), "ChatGPT=0.1% daily global words" (Dohmatob2024), "LAION-5B 已含合成图" (Alemohammad)。高。
22	
23	**命名现象**: "MAD cow disease" (Alemohammad) 是领域最高频引用 hook。命名让概念可传播: "MAD", "Strong Model Collapse", "generalization-to-memorization transition"。**4/8**。中。
24	
25	**直接挑战范式**: "Within the paradigm underpinning ChatGPT and Llama..." (Dohmatob2025)——对标 scaling law 提升 stakes。**2/8**。中。
26	
27	## Intro 结构
28	
29	**3-5 段**: P1=危机/背景, P2=前人+局限, P3=洞察, P4=贡献。**正好 3 条贡献**是标准。动词领头: "We identify/establish/propose"。顺序: 观察→机制→方法。**6/8**。高。
30	
31	## Figure 1 设计
32	
33	**两派分野**: teaser-result (3/8: Alemohammad 退化人脸, Dohmatob2025 Pareto 图) vs method-overview (3/8: He, Guo, Ba pipeline)。发现型论文用 teaser；方法型用 overview。高。
34	
35	## 结果可视化
36	
37	**Line plot 统治一切** (8/8): x=迭代/tokens, y=指标, 多条件曲线。理论论文叠加虚线预测在实线实验上——"pixel-perfect match" 是金标准。来源: Dohmatob2024, 2025。高。
38	
39	**t-SNE 可视化 collapse** (3/8): scatter plot 展示跨代空间压缩。用作定量指标的补充证据。来源: Guo, Alemohammad。中。
40	
41	**双证据面板** (5/8): 定性 (生成样本/t-SNE) + 定量 (曲线) 同框。He2025 Fig2 (最近邻+GS曲线), Dohmatob2024 Fig4 (scaling+skill)。高。
42	
43	## Figure 叙事弧
44	
45	**典型编排** (7/8): motivation/teaser → method → main results → ablation。来源: He2025, Alemohammad2024。高。
46	
47	## Table 设计
48	
49	**领域以图为主** — 5/8 论文零编号表格。有表时: 方法为行, 数据集×指标为列, bold-best。消融用曲线图 (6/8) 而非表格——连续参数以曲线呈现。高。
50	
51	## 关键修辞技法
52	
53	**量化最低阈值**: "as little as 1%" (Dohmatob2025) 比展示 100% 崩溃更有记忆点。高。
54	
55	**理论-实验叠加**: 虚线理论 + 实线实验同轴。匹配越精确论文越强。**3/8 理论论文**。高。
56	
57	**生物学/物理学类比**: "mad cow disease" (Alemohammad), "phase transition" (Truong), "autophagy" (Alemohammad)——跨领域类比提升可传播性。**3/8**。中。
58	
59	**B-V-ζ 分解可视化** (Dohmatob2025): 将总误差拆成 Bias/Variance/Collapse 三条曲线，读者一眼看出哪个分量造成问题。适合有理论贡献的论文模仿。中。
60

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01XQg4i9v5DtCe5pRacm3RV6`</summary>

````text
1	---
2	name: paper-writing-norms
3	description: 顶会级别论文写作风格规范——章节写法（Abstract/Intro/Related Work）、去 AI 风格、视觉密度、叙事哲学。Paper Agent 和 Worker 写论文章节时必须加载。
4	allowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]
5	scope: global
6	version: 2
7	priority: 9
8	tags:
9	  - writing
10	  - paper
11	  - style
12	  - norms
13	roles:
14	  - agent
15	  - worker
16	  - paper_agent
17	---
18	
19	# 论文写作风格规范
20	
21	> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。
22	
23	---
24	
25	## 一、总体写作哲学
26	
27	### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌
28	
29	- 论文必须以"故事线"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证
30	- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进
31	- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌
32	- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing
33	
34	### 1.2 主线统一性
35	
36	- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**
37	- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C
38	- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机
39	- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线
40	
41	### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义
42	
43	- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释
44	- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）
45	- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用
46	- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug
47	
48	### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文
49	
50	- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可
51	- 重点学：
52	  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事
53	  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献
54	  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）
55	  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度
56	- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大
57	- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文
58	
59	---
60	
61	## 二、Abstract 写作规范
62	
63	### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）
64	
65	Abstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：
66	1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点
67	2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）
68	3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）
69	4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献
70	
71	### 2.2 禁忌
72	
73	- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim
74	- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 "revolutionary"、"groundbreaking"
75	- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元
76	- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了
77	- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开
78	
79	### 2.3 审稿人视角检验
80	
81	写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——
82	- 问题是否重要？
83	- 方法是否清楚？
84	- 贡献是否可信？
85	- 结果是否有说服力？
86	
87	---
88	
89	## 三、Introduction 写作规范
90	
91	### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）
92	
93	Introduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：
94	
95	1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈
96	2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题
97	3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）
98	4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题
99	5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献
100	
101	### 3.2 整体叙事结构
102	
103	```
104	现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结
105	```
106	
107	### 3.3 禁忌
108	
109	- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 "LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities..."，要从具体问题切入
110	- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接
111	- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 "我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果" 的罗列
112	- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑
113	- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念
114	
115	---
116	
117	## 四、Related Work 写作规范
118	
119	### 4.1 目标
120	
121	让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。
122	
123	### 4.2 结构要求
124	
125	- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）
126	- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系
127	- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口
128	
129	### 4.3 禁忌
130	
131	- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 "A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z." 的列表
132	- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较
133	- Contribution claim（"To our knowledge, no prior work..."）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work
134	
135	### 4.4 引用充足度
136	
137	- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**
138	- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分
139	- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足
140	
141	---
142	
143	## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）
144	
145	以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**
146	
147	### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）
148	
149	**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**
150	
151	- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**
152	- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子
153	- 强制检查命令：
154	  ```bash
155	  grep -n "—" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要
156	  grep -n " – " paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除
157	  ```
158	- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）
159	
160	### 5.2 段落与句式
161	
162	- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的
163	- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句
164	- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 "..., highlighting the applicability of..." / "..., demonstrating the effectiveness of..."。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一
165	- **禁止连续使用 "Furthermore" / "Moreover" / "Additionally"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词
166	- **避免 "It is worth noting that..." / "Notably, ..."**：直接写要说的内容
167	
168	### 5.3 用词黑名单
169	
170	- 避免 "delve into" / "leverage" / "utilize" / "facilitate" — 用 "explore" / "use" / "enable"
171	- 避免 "a myriad of" / "a plethora of" — 用 "many" / "various"
172	- 避免 "in the realm of" / "in the context of" — 直接说
173	- 避免 "paradigm shift" / "novel" 过度使用
174	
175	### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）
176	
177	1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写
178	2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确
179	3. **`grep "—"` + `grep " – "` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）
180	4. `grep "ing,"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写
181	
182	---
183	
184	## 六、图表与视觉规范
185	
186	### 6.1 知识密度原则
187	
188	- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间
189	- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中
190	- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure
191	
192	### 6.2 表格规范
193	
194	- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）
195	- 小数精度全表统一
196	- 最优加粗、次优下划线
197	- Caption 必须自包含
198	
199	### 6.3 图片规范
200	
201	- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签
202	- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）
203	- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求
204	- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页
205	
206	### 6.4 空间效率
207	
208	- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源
209	- 优先使用 `\subfigure` 合并相关图
210	- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动
211	- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比
212	
213	### 6.5 页面节奏
214	
215	好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：
216	
217	- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉"三步展开
218	- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏
219	- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float
220	- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）
221	- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）
222	
223	### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范
224	
225	**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：
226	- 用 `\toprule` / `\midrule` / `\bottomrule`，禁止 `\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`
227	- 用 `\centering` 不用 `\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）
228	- 最优结果加粗（`\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向
229	- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）
230	- Caption 在表格上方（`\caption` 在 `\begin{tabular}` 之前）
231	
232	**宽度决策树**：
233	- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\columnwidth`）
234	- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）
235	- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix
236	
237	---
238	
239	## 七、投稿前通用准备
240	
241	### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）
242	
243	- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）
244	- **不要用**：
245	  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）
246	  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）
247	  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）
248	- 风险：
249	  - 样式不符合 → desk reject
250	  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信
251	  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范
252	- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch "{venue} {year} latex template official"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）
253	- `\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致
254	
255	### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对
256	
257	- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A
258	- 页数限制：查目标会议官网
259	- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同
260	- 行号开启（review 版常需）
261	- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件
262	
263	### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程
264	
265	提交前必须逐一检查：
266	1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入
267	2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态
268	3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事
269	4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）
270	5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）
271	6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）
272	7. 所有专有名词有定义
273	8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）
274	9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡
275	10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础
276	
````

</details>

      "content": "---\nname: paper-writing-norms\ndescription: 顶会级别论文写作风格规范——章节写法（Abstract/Intro/Related Work）、去 AI 风格、视觉密度、叙事哲学。Paper Agent 和 Worker 写论文章节时必须加载。\nallowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]\nscope: global\nversion: 2\npriority: 9\ntags:\n  - writing\n  - paper\n  - style\n  - norms\nroles:\n  - agent\n  - worker\n  - paper_agent\n---\n\n# 论文写作风格规范\n\n> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。\n\n---\n\n## 一、总体写作哲学\n\n### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌\n\n- 论文必须以\"故事线\"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证\n- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进\n- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌\n- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing\n\n### 1.2 主线统一性\n\n- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**\n- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C\n- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机\n- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线\n\n### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义\n\n- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释\n- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）\n- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用\n- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug\n\n### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文\n\n- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可\n- 重点学：\n  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事\n  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献\n  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）\n  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度\n- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大\n- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文\n\n---\n\n## 二、Abstract 写作规范\n\n### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）\n\nAbstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：\n1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点\n2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）\n3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）\n4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献\n\n### 2.2 禁忌\n\n- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim\n- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 \"revolutionary\"、\"groundbreaking\"\n- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元\n- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了\n- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开\n\n### 2.3 审稿人视角检验\n\n写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——\n- 问题是否重要？\n- 方法是否清楚？\n- 贡献是否可信？\n- 结果是否有说服力？\n\n---\n\n## 三、Introduction 写作规范\n\n### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）\n\nIntroduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：\n\n1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈\n2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题\n3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）\n4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题\n5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献\n\n### 3.2 整体叙事结构\n\n```\n现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结\n```\n\n### 3.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 \"LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities...\"，要从具体问题切入\n- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接\n- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 \"我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果\" 的罗列\n- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑\n- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念\n\n---\n\n## 四、Related Work 写作规范\n\n### 4.1 目标\n\n让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。\n\n### 4.2 结构要求\n\n- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）\n- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系\n- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口\n\n### 4.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 \"A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z.\" 的列表\n- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较\n- Contribution claim（\"To our knowledge, no prior work...\"）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work\n\n### 4.4 引用充足度\n\n- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**\n- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分\n- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足\n\n---\n\n## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）\n\n以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**\n\n### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n\n**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**\n\n- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**\n- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子\n- 强制检查命令：\n  ```bash\n  grep -n \"—\" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要\n  grep -n \" – \" paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除\n  ```\n- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）\n\n### 5.2 段落与句式\n\n- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的\n- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句\n- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 \"..., highlighting the applicability of...\" / \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一\n- **禁止连续使用 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词\n- **避免 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"**：直接写要说的内容\n\n### 5.3 用词黑名单\n\n- 避免 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"facilitate\" — 用 \"explore\" / \"use\" / \"enable\"\n- 避免 \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\" — 用 \"many\" / \"various\"\n- 避免 \"in the realm of\" / \"in the context of\" — 直接说\n- 避免 \"paradigm shift\" / \"novel\" 过度使用\n\n### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）\n\n1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写\n2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确\n3. **`grep \"—\"` + `grep \" – \"` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）\n4. `grep \"ing,\"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写\n\n---\n\n## 六、图表与视觉规范\n\n### 6.1 知识密度原则\n\n- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间\n- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中\n- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure\n\n### 6.2 表格规范\n\n- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）\n- 小数精度全表统一\n- 最优加粗、次优下划线\n- Caption 必须自包含\n\n### 6.3 图片规范\n\n- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签\n- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）\n- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求\n- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页\n\n### 6.4 空间效率\n\n- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源\n- 优先使用 `\\subfigure` 合并相关图\n- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动\n- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比\n\n### 6.5 页面节奏\n\n好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：\n\n- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用\"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉\"三步展开\n- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏\n- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float\n- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）\n- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）\n\n### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范\n\n**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：\n- 用 `\\toprule` / `\\midrule` / `\\bottomrule`，禁止 `\\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`\n- 用 `\\centering` 不用 `\\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）\n- 最优结果加粗（`\\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向\n- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）\n- Caption 在表格上方（`\\caption` 在 `\\begin{tabular}` 之前）\n\n**宽度决策树**：\n- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\\columnwidth`）\n- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）\n- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix\n\n---\n\n## 七、投稿前通用准备\n\n### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）\n\n- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）\n- **不要用**：\n  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）\n  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）\n  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）\n- 风险：\n  - 样式不符合 → desk reject\n  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信\n  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范\n- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch \"{venue} {year} latex template official\"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）\n- `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致\n\n### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对\n\n- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A\n- 页数限制：查目标会议官网\n- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同\n- 行号开启（review 版常需）\n- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件\n\n### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程\n\n提交前必须逐一检查：\n1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入\n2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态\n3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事\n4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）\n5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）\n6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）\n7. 所有专有名词有定义\n8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）\n9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡\n10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础\n",
      "numLines": 276,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 276
    }

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	When language models train iteratively on their own output, distributional degradation proceeds silently until collapse is advanced.
3	We map the detectability landscape of this process primarily on Pythia-410M across 13 contamination levels, with cross-architecture validation on five families (345M--6.9B parameters), revealing blind zones, sharp detection onsets, and a non-monotonic dip where Granger detection fails despite confirmed causal flow.
4	Permutation Granger causality\footnote{Granger precedence establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causation~\cite{shojaie2022granger}.} (PermG) identifies a detection boundary $\alpha_c(T)$ following a power law in observation length (leave-one-$T$-out CV: MAE${=}0.037$, 15/15 cells within CI): any nonzero contamination produces signal, but sub-linear growth makes detection a function of $T$.
5	This landscape is profoundly method-dependent: five statistical methods applied to identical data yield null-control false positive rates from 3\% (FDR-corrected) to 92\% (uncorrected).
6	Under mode amplification, entropy Granger-precedes diversity collapse with a ${\geq}1$-generation lag (confirmed in 14 of 15 experiments), providing a canary signal whose triggered cessation reduces peak perplexity as a proof-of-concept intervention.
7	\end{abstract}
8	
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	When language models train iteratively on their own output, the training distribution progressively loses its tail modes, a phenomenon termed model collapse~\cite{shumailov2024curse,alemohammad2024mad}.
5	Lexical diversity narrows, calibration deteriorates, and each generation inherits the systematic biases of its predecessors.
6	As machine-generated text becomes prevalent on the web, such feedback loops pose a growing risk to production training pipelines.
7	By the time standard quality metrics register degradation, the underlying distribution may have already shifted irreversibly.
8	
9	This threat motivates a fundamental question: \emph{what does the detectability landscape of model collapse look like?}
10	At what contamination level does statistical detection become possible, how does this boundary depend on observation length and statistical method, and what structure does the landscape exhibit?
11	Iterative training produces short, noisy time series (often fewer than 15 points per generation sequence), where detection power drops sharply~\cite{boettiger2012limits}, and existing analyses leave these dependencies unresolved.
12	Our detection mechanism (Granger temporal precedence) differs from critical-slowing-down indicators, as model collapse is a first-order transition~\cite{truong2026phase} (\S\ref{sec:related_work}).
13	
14	We systematically map this landscape using Pythia-410M~\cite{biderman2023pythia} across 13 contamination levels (including null control) with multi-seed replication, applying five statistical methods from econometrics~\cite{granger1974spurious,toda1995statistical} to identical data.
15	The resulting map reveals structured terrain (Figure~\ref{fig:regime_map}): blind zones where collapse proceeds undetected, a sharp onset where detection jumps from null to reliable within a 2-percentage-point interval, a non-monotonic dip where Granger detection fails despite confirmed causal flow, and a detection boundary $\alpha_c(T)$ that follows a power law in observation length.
16	Cross-architecture validation across five families (345M--6.9B) supports that the phase-diagram structure generalizes above the detection boundary when entropy-diversity coupling is preserved.
17	Granger precedence establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causality~\cite{shojaie2022granger}; our canary claim is scoped to diversity metrics (distinct-$n$~\cite{li2016diversity}).
18	
19	Our contributions are:
20	\begin{enumerate}
21	    \item \textbf{Detectability landscape characterization} (\S\ref{sec:regime}--\ref{sec:intervention}).
22	    PermG establishes a monotonically decreasing detection boundary $\alpha_c(T)$ following a power law in observation length (Proposition~\ref{prop:phase-boundary}), primarily characterized on Pythia-410M; leave-one-$T$-out CV confirms predictive accuracy (MAE${=}0.037$, CI~$15/15$; \S\ref{sec:theory}).
23	    Since $\alpha_\infty{\approx}0$, any nonzero contamination produces signal, but sub-linear growth makes detection a function of $T$.
24	    Predict-then-verify at $T{=}20$ matches 3/5 predictions in saturated regimes, with systematic conservative bias at boundary $\alpha$ (Appendix~\ref{app:t20_prediction_timeline}); canary-triggered cessation reduces peak perplexity by 64\%.
25	
26	    \item \textbf{Formal motivation for canary metric selection} (\S\ref{sec:cascade}).
27	    Under mode amplification, entropy Granger-precedes diversity (Proposition~\ref{thm:entropy_leads}), with a ${\geq}1$-generation lag (Proposition~\ref{prop:div_lag}), confirmed in 14/15 experiments ($\alpha \geq 0.30$; GPT-2-medium s43 bidirectional).
28	
29	    \item \textbf{Method sensitivity as a primary finding} (\S\ref{sec:mmct}--\ref{sec:dose_response}).
30	    Five detection methods yield null-control FPR from 3\% (FDR-corrected) to 92\% (uncorrected); the same Granger test shifts from 0/8 to 6/8 within a 2-percentage-point interval.
31	    The detectability landscape one observes depends critically on the statistical method used to probe it.
32	\end{enumerate}
33	
34	\begin{figure*}[t]
35	\centering
36	\includegraphics[width=0.55\textwidth]{figures/fig_phase_diagram.pdf}
37	\caption{Detection phase diagram (Pythia-410M, fp32, PermG direction-checked). Each cell shows the number of FDR-significant canary$\to$diversity pairs (of~8) at a given contamination strength~$\alpha$ and observation window~$T$. The dashed curve is the theoretical detection boundary $\alpha_c(T) \propto T^{-\gamma}$ with $\gamma{=}2.08$, fitted (Proposition~\ref{prop:phase-boundary}). Diamond markers ($\diamond$) flag the synchronous dip at $\alpha{=}0.60$ (0/8 at $T{\leq}20$); the star ($\star$) marks its dissolution at $T{=}25$ (5/8), validating $\sqrt{T}$ power scaling.}
38	\label{fig:regime_map}
39	\end{figure*}
40	
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Model collapse and canary metrics.}
5	Iterative training collapse was formalized by \citet{shumailov2024curse} and \citet{alemohammad2024mad}, who showed that recursively replacing real data with model-generated text causes progressive tail erosion; \citet{dey2024breaking} demonstrated that data accumulation (rather than replacement) can mitigate collapse; \citet{schaeffer2025position} systematize eight distinct notions of ``collapse'' that this literature conflates.
6	Our operationalization---declining detection rate of Granger-precedence canary$\to$diversity links across generations---aligns most closely with Schaeffer et al.'s \emph{distributional shift} family (definitions~D4--D6 in their taxonomy), measuring how the generated distribution departs from the real-data baseline rather than tracking population risk or scaling-law exponents.
7	Theoretical analyses have sharpened these results: \citet{dohmatob2024tale} recast collapse as a change in scaling-law exponents, \citet{dohmatob2025strong} proved that collapse can occur even at vanishingly small synthetic fractions, \citet{seddik2025statistical} provide a statistical analysis of iterative contamination dynamics, and \citet{marchi2025challenging} challenge the universality of collapse under diverse training conditions.
8	Several works have proposed monitoring metrics: \citet{du2025scales} report ECE increases at 30\% contamination before accuracy drops, \citet{gambetta2024surplexity} introduces surplexity as a collapse detector, \citet{drayson2025detection} show that machine-generated text detection can prevent collapse, and \citet{li2025verification} propose verification-based mitigation.
9	\citet{shi2025closer} independently validate entropy as a collapse diagnostic in diffusion models ($r{=}0.91$ with generalization score), using it for diversity-aware sample selection.
10	\citet{du2026geometric} address a related but distinct phenomenon---mode collapse during generation---via geometric regulation of the representation trajectory; our work instead studies \emph{iterative training} collapse across generations.
11	We differ from prior monitoring work in four respects (comparison table: Appendix~\ref{app:method_comparison}): (i)~we compare an entire family of canary metrics; (ii)~we cross-validate across five statistical methods; (iii)~we map detectability as a function of contamination regime; and (iv)~we treat method-selection sensitivity itself as a primary finding.
12	
13	\paragraph{Data mixing phase transitions.}
14	\citet{kazdan2025collapse} map the conditions under which synthetic data triggers collapse versus improves performance, \citet{gu2024cmr} establish a scaling law for critical mixture ratios in continual pre-training, and \citet{gu2025datamixing} show that data mixing can induce phase transitions in knowledge acquisition---a direct theoretical analogue to our detection threshold, where a critical mixing ratio separates qualitatively different detection regimes.
15	
16	\paragraph{Critical transitions and lead-lag methodology.}
17	\citet{scheffer2009early} established that systems approaching tipping points exhibit generic early-warning signals (rising autocorrelation and variance); \citet{dakos2012methods} developed a practical toolkit for estimating such signals, while \citet{boettiger2012limits} quantified detection limits in short time series---directly relevant to iterative training where $T{=}5$--$20$.
18	Crucially, \citet{truong2026phase} prove that entropy collapse in iterative training is a first-order (discontinuous) phase transition, structurally ruling out critical-slowing-down (CSD) early-warning signals.
19	Our Granger-precedence approach circumvents this limitation by detecting temporal predictive improvement rather than variance/autocorrelation signatures, achieving ${\geq}5/8$ forward detection in 12 of 15 above-boundary cells (three sync-dip exceptions at $\alpha{=}0.60$ dissolve by $T{=}25$; \S\ref{sec:t20}).
20	The standard framework for testing temporal precedence is Granger causality~\cite{granger1969investigating}, with neural extensions for nonlinear settings~\citep{tank2021neural,marcinkevics2022neural,zeng2025kangci}; however, non-stationary series inflate rejection rates~\cite{granger1974spurious}, and the Toda--Yamamoto procedure~\cite{toda1995statistical} addresses this at reduced degrees of freedom severe in short series.
21	We use classical rather than neural Granger methods because $T{\leq}25$ provides insufficient observations for neural network parameter estimation.
22	These methods are largely absent from the model collapse literature.
23	Our analysis studies iterative from-scratch retraining; online or continual learning settings, where the model updates incrementally on streaming data, may exhibit different detection dynamics.
24	
25	\paragraph{Analytical robustness.}
26	\citet{bell2022modeling} introduced multiverse analysis to ML, \citet{coretta2023multidimensional} demonstrated that 46 teams reached different conclusions on identical data, and \citet{kapoor2024reforms} synthesized these concerns into a consensus checklist.
27	Our work applies this perspective to model collapse: five methods across multiple contamination regimes produce conclusions ranging from universal significance to complete null.
28	
```

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\paragraph{Design considerations.}
5	\label{sec:pitfalls}
6	Lead-lag analysis in iterative model collapse is fragile along three axes, each documented with data from \S\ref{sec:regime}--\ref{sec:dose_response}.
7	\emph{Method sensitivity}: five detection methods applied to identical data yield forward significance rates spanning 0\% to 92\%, with inter-method agreement at $\kappa{=}0.185$.
8	\emph{Spurious regression}: first-differencing eliminates canary$\to$perplexity associations ($F{\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$) while preserving canary$\to$diversity (24/24$\to$24/24), exposing an entire analysis channel as trend-driven~\cite{granger1974spurious}.
9	\emph{Regime dependence}: the same Granger test shifts from 0/8 to 6/8 significant pairs within a 2-percentage-point interval ($\alpha{=}0.48 \to 0.50$).
10	
11	\subsection{Method Sensitivity Analysis}
12	\label{sec:mmct}
13	We compare five lead-lag detection methods---naive cross-correlation, differenced cross-correlation, threshold onset, permutation Granger causality (PermG), and Toda--Yamamoto~\cite{toda1995statistical}---in a multi-method comparison~\cite{simonsohn2020specification} (algorithm in Appendix~\ref{app:mmct}) calibrated against null-control ($\alpha{=}0.0$) data where any detection is a false positive.
14	Detection results use equal-weight majority voting ($K{\geq}3$ of 5 methods, each contributing one vote per pair) with forward-direction filtering; FPR-inverse weighting is used only in exploratory method ranking (Appendix~\ref{app:weight_robustness}).
15	Each method embodies different assumptions: naive cross-correlation is assumption-free but produces 92\% null-control FPR; differenced cross-correlation controls for trends but lacks directional separation; threshold onset is power-limited at $T{=}11$; Toda--Yamamoto avoids pre-testing for unit roots; PermG uses a nonparametric permutation null with built-in directionality.
16	We classify a method as \emph{directional} if its test structure inherently separates forward from reverse precedence---PermG tests each direction independently; threshold onset compares crossing order---rather than inferring direction post-hoc from peak lag sign (NaiveXCorr, DiffXCorr).
17	Pooled null-control FPR across six seeds (6-pair basis, 36 pairs): DiffXCorr 2.8\%, PermG 11.1\% (lowest among directional methods; cluster-adjusted 95\% CI $[1.2\%, 30.4\%]$), Threshold 27.8\%, Toda--Yamamoto 38.9\%, NaiveXCorr 91.7\% (Appendix~\ref{app:fpr_independence}).
18	Under identical BH-FDR correction at $q{=}0.05$, DiffXCorr achieves the lowest FPR (1/36, 2.8\%); PermG (4/36, 11.1\%) is recommended for its directional specificity, as DiffXCorr is bidirectional---it cannot distinguish forward from reverse precedence---making it unsuitable as a standalone collapse detector.
19	Under all four weighting schemes (equal, FPR-inverse, capped, rank-based), PermG achieves the lowest null-control FPR among directionally-specific methods alongside consistent detection at $\alpha \geq 0.50$ (Shapley analysis: Appendix~\ref{app:shapley}).
20	Practitioner-facing alternatives (PPL-slope, variance thresholds) yield 67--100\% FPR under natural training drift (Appendix~\ref{app:naive_baselines}).
21	This finding---that method choice alone can shift conclusions from universal significance to complete null on identical data---motivates reporting method sensitivity as a primary result rather than relegating it to a robustness check.
22	
23	\paragraph{Permutation Granger causality.}
24	PermG tests whether past values of canary metric $X$ improve prediction of downstream metric $Y$ beyond $Y$'s own history; Granger precedence establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causality~\cite{shojaie2022granger}.
25	We use classical rather than neural Granger methods~\citep{tank2021neural} because $T{\leq}25$ provides insufficient observations for neural network parameter estimation.
26	We apply 5{,}000 permutations~\citep{phipson2010permutation} on first-differenced series with Benjamini--Hochberg FDR correction at $q{=}0.05$ across 8 canary$\to$downstream pairs (2 canaries $\times$ 4 downstream metrics, sharing canary terms and thus positively dependent).
27	The permutation test controls Type~I error exactly by construction (exchangeable null distribution); BH-FDR remains valid under positive regression dependence on a subset (PRDS)~\cite{benjamini2001control}, and 4/36 null-control rejections across six seeds yield the lowest empirical FPR among directionally-specific methods (11.1\%; DiffXCorr achieves 2.8\% but lacks directional separation; Clopper--Pearson 95\% CI $[3.1\%, 26.1\%]$ under independence; within-seed ICC${=}0.14$, cluster-adjusted 95\% CI $[1.2\%, 30.4\%]$; Appendix~\ref{app:fpr_independence}).
28	Simulation under non-stationary AR(1) DGP ($\varphi{=}0.95$--$0.99$) reveals PermG FPR inflation to ${\sim}10\%$; however, our empirical series exhibit low stochastic persistence (null token entropy $\varphi{=}-0.51$; first-differenced distinct-1 $\varphi{<}0.44$), and 4/36 null rejections (11.1\%, lowest among directional methods) confirm adequate size control (Appendix~\ref{app:nonstationarity}).
29	The same simulation shows PermG retains ${\sim}80\%$ of raw-levels power at $T{=}11$ versus ${\sim}61\%$ for standard first-differencing, justifying the permutation approach at short series lengths (see also Appendices~\ref{app:robustness}, \ref{app:power_analysis}, \ref{app:transfer_entropy}).
30	
31	\subsection{Detection Boundary}
32	\label{sec:theory}
33	
34	We model the PermG detection rate as a $T$-dependent steep sigmoid, excluding the non-monotonic dip at $\alpha \in [0.55, 0.65]$:
35	\begin{equation}
36	\mathrm{rate}(\alpha, T) = \frac{0.70}{1 + \exp\!\bigl(-100\,(\alpha - \alpha_c(T))\bigr)},
37	\label{eq:joint-sigmoid}
38	\end{equation}
39	fitted to $T{=}10$ data ($n{=}11$, sync-dip excluded; $R^2{=}0.969$).
40	The detection boundary follows a power law in observation length:
41	\begin{equation}
42	\alpha_c(T) = A \cdot T^{-\gamma},
43	\label{eq:alpha-c}
44	\end{equation}
45	where the exponent $\gamma$ is determined by the sub-linear signal model below.
46	
47	\begin{proposition}[Empirical detection boundary]\label{prop:phase-boundary}
48	Under an empirical sub-linear signal model $S(\alpha) = c \cdot \alpha^\beta$ with $\beta < 1$, the detection boundary satisfies
49	\begin{equation}
50	\alpha_c(T) = A \cdot T^{-1/(2\beta)},
51	\label{eq:alpha-c-theory}
52	\end{equation}
53	where $A = (z^* \sigma / c)^{1/\beta}$ and $z^* = \Phi^{-1}(\pi^*)$ \textup{(proof in Appendix~\ref{app:alpha_c_proof})}.
54	\end{proposition}
55	
56	\noindent The signal model $S(\alpha) = c\alpha^\beta$ is an empirical parametrization: $\beta$ is fitted from data rather than derived from training dynamics.
57	The power-law form $\alpha_c \propto T^{-1/(2\beta)}$ follows from signal-detection theory (signal-to-noise crossing a threshold), but $\beta$ requires architecture-specific calibration.
58	
59	Cross-$T$ validation (train $T{\in}\{10,20\}$, test $T{\in}\{5,15\}$) yields $\beta{=}0.24$, $\gamma{=}2.08$ (MAE${=}0.106$, CI $6/6$); leave-one-$T$-out CV on the full grid ($T{\in}\{5,10,15,20,25\}$, sync-dip excluded) confirms predictive accuracy (MAE${=}0.037$, CI $15/15$; Appendix~\ref{app:alpha_c_proof}).
60	However, $\beta$ is poorly identified: MLE $\hat{\beta}{=}0.66$ with profile 95\% CI $[0.24, 0.78]$, and model comparison favors the power-probit (M2) over a linear boundary (M1) only when sync-dip data are included ($\Delta\mathrm{AIC}{=}4.15$ without; Appendix~\ref{app:alpha_c_proof}).
61	
62	\paragraph{Shape comparison with smooth alternatives.}
63	A concave signal model ($\beta'{=}0.5$) produces $\gamma'{=}1.87$, close to the observed $2.08$, so the scaling exponent alone cannot distinguish smooth from steep-sigmoid boundaries.
64	Formal shape testing on dense $T{=}10$ data (17~$\alpha$ values) favors a smooth sigmoid over a literal step function ($\Delta\mathrm{AIC}{=}-110$), but the fitted onset steepness ($k{=}15.7$, 95\% CI $[9.4, 28.2]$; \S\ref{sec:dose_response}) is $3$--$4{\times}$ higher than any smooth signal model predicts.
65	VAR(1) power simulations (1{,}000 per cell; Appendix~\ref{app:power_sim}) under H0 (linear) and H0$'$ (concave) produce onset sigmoids with $k{\approx}5$--$6$, yielding $k_{\mathrm{obs}} / k_{\mathrm{H0}} \approx 3$ (CI lower bound $k{=}9.4$, ratio ${\approx}2$).
66	This steepness gap indicates a significantly sharper transition than statistical power alone can produce, consistent with a genuine but finite-width phase boundary rather than a gradual power artifact.
67	Since $\alpha_\infty{\approx}0$, any nonzero contamination produces signal ($S \propto \alpha^{0.24}$), but sub-linear growth makes detection a function of $T$.
68

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We study lead-lag analysis design considerations in iterative training collapse using Pythia-410M~\cite{biderman2023pythia} (410M parameters, decoder-only), with cross-architecture validation on GPT-2-medium (345M) and three additional families (\S\ref{sec:generalization}).
8	Training data is drawn from WikiText-103~\cite{merity2017pointer} following the replace paradigm of~\citet{shumailov2024curse}: each generation fine-tunes on a mixture of $\alpha$ synthetic and $(1{-}\alpha)$ real data, generates 5{,}000 replacement samples, and fully replaces the synthetic pool (hyperparameters in Appendix~\ref{app:training_details}; notation in Appendix~\ref{app:background}).
9	We run $T{=}10$ generations across 13 contamination levels ($\alpha \in \{0.00, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.30, 0.35, 0.40, 0.45, 0.50, 0.55, 0.60, 0.75, 1.00\}$, including null control) with multi-seed replication (4 seeds at $\alpha{=}1.0$; 3 at primary levels).
10	
11	We track two \emph{canary metrics}---token entropy and ECE---and three \emph{downstream metrics}: distinct-1, distinct-2~\cite{li2016diversity}, and perplexity (distinct-3: Appendix~\ref{app:distinct3}).
12	All hypothesis tests apply BH-FDR correction at $q{=}0.05$; PermG uses 5{,}000 permutations on first-differenced series~\cite{shojaie2022granger} with maximum lag~5 (window sensitivity: Appendix~\ref{app:window_sensitivity}).
13	
14	
15	\subsection{Sharp Detection Onset}
16	\label{sec:regime}
17	
18	Diversity collapse occurs at every contamination level: distinct-1 declines by $14.1\%$ at $\alpha{=}0.0$ (fine-tuning drift) and by $40.3{\pm}0.3\%$ at $\alpha{=}0.25$ (3-seed mean).
19	Despite this ubiquitous degradation, Granger detectability is sharply regime-dependent.
20	The onset we report reflects PermG's statistical power curve crossing the detection threshold at a critical contamination level, not the emergence of a new physical signal; this interpretation is confirmed by sub-threshold $p$-values at $\alpha{=}0.45$ (\S\ref{sec:theory}).
21	Permutation Granger (PermG) tests find 0/8 canary$\to$downstream pairs FDR-significant from $\alpha{=}0.00$ through $\alpha{=}0.45$, then jump to 6/8 at $\alpha{=}0.50$ uniformly across all 3~fp32 seeds.
22	This transition is striking because cumulative synthetic fraction barely changes across the boundary: $C = 1{-}(1{-}\alpha)^{10}$ yields $99.75\%$ at $\alpha{=}0.45$ versus $99.90\%$ at $\alpha{=}0.50$, a difference of 0.15 percentage points, yet detection jumps from null to reliable.
23	Sigmoid regression on the 13-point $T{=}10$ grid yields $\alpha_c = 0.483 \pm 0.006$ ($R^2 = 0.969$); dense $\alpha$ sampling (17~values) constrains the onset steepness to $k{=}15.7$ (95\% CI $[9.4, 28.2]$), a steep but finite-width sigmoid (Figure~\ref{fig:dose_response}).
24	This single-$T$ estimate is consistent with the cross-$T$ validated model (\S\ref{sec:theory}), which predicts how the detection boundary shifts with observation length ($\gamma{=}2.08$).
25	The transition is not a literal step function ($\Delta\mathrm{AIC}{=}-110$ favoring sigmoid; Appendix~\ref{app:sigmoid_shape}), but its steepness (${\approx}3{\times}$ smooth-model predictions; \S\ref{sec:theory}) exceeds what PermG power curves alone produce.
26	At $T{=}10$, PermG power is ${\approx}37\%$ (Appendix~\ref{app:power_sim}), partly amplifying the $0/8{\to}6/8$ sharpness; however, onset steepness ($k{=}15.7$, ${\approx}3{\times}$ smooth predictions) and cross-seed consistency (3/3 seeds: $0/8{\to}6/8$ at $\alpha{=}0.48{\to}0.50$) argue for a genuine, power-modulated transition.
27	A Fisher exact test corroborates the categorical jump: 0/64 pairs FDR-significant at $\alpha \leq 0.45$ versus 18/32 at $\alpha \geq 0.50$ ($p = 3.45 \times 10^{-11}$); sub-threshold $p$-values at $\alpha{=}0.45$ ($p_{\mathrm{fdr}} = 0.059$--$0.091$) are consistent with proximity to the power boundary (\S\ref{sec:theory}).
28	A systematic robustness analysis (1{,}160 configurations) confirms specification-invariance of the onset.
29	
30	\paragraph{Canary scoping.}
31	First-differencing scopes the canary claim precisely.
32	Canary$\to$diversity pairs retain unanimous FDR significance after first-differencing ($24/24 \to 24/24$ across 4~seeds at $\alpha{=}1.0$, 6 pairs per seed), while canary$\to$perplexity collapses entirely ($F \approx 0$, $p = 0.99$): the apparent perplexity association is a shared-trend artifact.
33	All canary claims are therefore scoped to diversity metrics only.
34	This scoping reflects a genuine temporal structure difference, not selective power loss from differencing: $|r|$ retention is ${\geq}95\%$ for both channels, but canary$\to$diversity correlations peak at lagged offsets (81\% at lag${<}0$, median${=}-1$) while canary$\to$perplexity correlations are contemporaneous (50\% at lag${=}0$), producing $20$--$28{\times}$ lower Granger $F$ for perplexity (Appendix~\ref{app:channel_effect_size}; perplexity $n{=}8$, limited sample).
35	At $\alpha{=}1.0$, all 24 canary$\to$diversity pairs (6 per seed $\times$ 4~seeds; mauve excluded) reach FDR significance in the forward direction with 0/24 reverse; directional asymmetry is robust across all WikiText conditions.
36	Independent of the Granger test, the degradation cascade confirms temporal precedence: entropy onset at generation~1 precedes distinct-$n$ onset at generation~2 in 16/18 WikiText conditions (Kendall $\tau{=}0.337$; \S\ref{sec:cascade}, Appendix~\ref{app:moment_cascade}), with supporting change-point analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:change_point}).
37	The canary mechanism also exhibits a blind spot: at $\alpha{=}0.10$, distinct-1 has already dropped by $39.6\%$, yet token entropy is indistinguishable from the null ($-5.3\%$ versus $-4.6{\pm}0.4\%$ at $\alpha{=}0.0$).
38	The gen-1 entropy deviation spans three orders of magnitude across the $\alpha$ range: $0.5\sigma$ at $\alpha{=}0.10$ to $60\sigma$ at $\alpha{=}0.75$ (relative to null variability), explaining why the blind zone persists despite increasing contamination.\footnote{Seed~43 at $\alpha{=}0.10$ shows 3/8 versus 0/8 for seed~42; no other sub-threshold level exhibits seed-dependent detection.}
39	Below $\alpha_c$ at $T{=}10$, diversity collapse proceeds without any detectable Granger-precedence signal in the canary metrics, though Gaussian MI remains positive (Appendix~\ref{app:mi_analysis}).
40	
41	\begin{figure*}[t]
42	\centering
43	\includegraphics[width=0.65\textwidth]{figures/metric_trajectory.pdf}
44	\caption{Metric trajectories at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (Pythia-410M, 4~seeds). Token entropy drops at generation~1; distinct-1 declines from generations~2--3, with a consistent one-generation canary lead across all four seeds (shaded: inter-seed range). This pattern corresponds to 24/24 forward and 0/24 reverse FDR-significant canary$\to$diversity pairs (6 per seed, mauve excluded).}
45	\label{fig:metric_trajectory}
46	\end{figure*}
47	
48	
49	\subsection{Dose-Response and Detection Regimes}
50	\label{sec:dose_response}
51	
52	\begin{figure}[t]
53	\centering
54	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_dose_response.pdf}
55	\caption{PermG dose-response profile (Pythia-410M, $T{=}10$, fp32). Sigmoid fit (shaded 95\% CI) yields $\alpha_c{=}0.483$. Four regimes: blind ($\alpha{\leq}0.45$, 0/8), onset ($\alpha{\approx}0.50$, 6/8), sync-dip ($\alpha{=}0.60$, 0/8), and detection ($\alpha{\geq}0.75$).}
56	\label{fig:dose_response}
57	\end{figure}
58	
59	The PermG dose-response profile (Figure~\ref{fig:dose_response}; full per-method table in Appendix~\ref{app:dose_response}) reveals four detection regimes: \emph{blind} ($\alpha \leq 0.45$, 0/8 FDR-significant pairs), \emph{onset} ($\alpha \approx 0.50$, 6/8), \emph{non-monotonic dip} ($\alpha \approx 0.60$, 0/8), and \emph{detection} ($\alpha{\geq}0.75$, 6/8 across 4~seeds at $\alpha{=}1.0$).
60	At $\alpha{=}0.55$, PermG returns 0/8 FDR-significant pairs despite marginal $p$-values ($p_{\mathrm{fdr}}{=}0.062$--$0.092$); differenced cross-correlation detects 8/8 bidirectionally at this level, confirming that a lead-lag signal exists but falls below PermG's directional detection threshold.
61	At $\alpha{=}0.60$, PermG returns $0/8$ at $T{\leq}20$ but recovers to $5/8$ at $T{=}25$, confirming a transient dip that $\sqrt{T}$ power eventually overcomes (Appendix~\ref{app:t20_comparison}).
62	
63	Method choice is itself a major source of variability: at $\alpha{=}1.0$, direction-checked forward significance spans 0\% to 75\% (Table~\ref{tab:method_comparison}), with $\kappa{=}0.185$; a specification curve (18~variants; Appendix~\ref{app:spec_curve}) confirms onset invariance.
64	Task degradation corroborates: perplexity jumps $+31\%$ between $\alpha{=}0.50$ and $0.52$ (Appendix~\ref{app:task_degradation}).
65	
66	
67	\begin{table}[t]
68	\centering
69	\small
70	\caption{Forward-direction FDR significance rates (\%) across contamination levels (Pythia-410M, seed~42, fp32; $3\sigma$ threshold calibration, lag-direction filtering for cross-correlation methods). Each cell: fraction of 8 validated pairs at BH-corrected significance ($q{=}0.05$). PermG achieves the lowest null-control FPR (11.1\% pooled across 6 seeds) with consistent detection at $\alpha \geq 0.50$.}
71	\label{tab:method_comparison}
72	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
73	\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}
74	\toprule
75	Method & $\alpha{=}0.0$ & $\alpha{=}0.25$ & $\alpha{=}0.50$ & $\alpha{=}0.75$ & $\alpha{=}1.0$ & FPR$_\text{null}$ \\
76	\midrule
77	Naive XCorr          & 75   & 62.5 & 62.5 & 0    & 0    & 75  \\
78	Diff.\ XCorr         & 0    & 25   & 12.5 & 0    & 0    & 0   \\
79	Threshold ($3\sigma$) & 37.5 & 62.5 & 100  & 100  & 25   & 37.5 \\
80	Perm.\ Granger       & \textbf{0}    & \textbf{0}    & \textbf{75}   & \textbf{75}   & \textbf{75}   & \textbf{0}   \\
81	Toda--Yamamoto$^\S$  & 12.5 & 37.5 & 50   & 100  & 12.5 & 12.5 \\
82	\bottomrule
83	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\footnotesize $^\S$TY directional assignment under verification; row retained for completeness.} \\
84	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\footnotesize Pooled FPR (3 seeds, cluster-bootstrap 95\% CI): \S\ref{sec:mmct}.} \\
85	\end{tabular}}
86	\end{table}
87	
88	
89	\subsection{Detection Boundary Left-Shift}
90	\label{sec:t20}
91	
92	The detection boundary $\alpha_c(T)$ decreases monotonically: $\alpha_c(5){\geq}1.0$, $\alpha_c(10){=}0.487$, consistent with $T^{-2.08}$ power scaling (\S\ref{sec:theory}).
93	The $\alpha{=}0.45$ column traces the sharpest validation: $0/8$ at $T{\leq}10$, $6/8$ at $T{=}15$ (dual-seed), $5/8$ at $T{=}20$, $6/8$ at $T{=}25$.
94	The systematic 2/8 gap reflects MAUVE floor saturation ($\mu{=}0.029$, SNR $4.68$ vs diversity $6.40$--$8.20$; \S\ref{sec:discussion}).
95	Cross-seed replication shows PermG within ${\pm}1$, though boundary cells exhibit sensitivity: $[0, 6]/8$ at ($T{=}10$, $\alpha{=}0.50$); $[0, 3]/8$ at ($T{=}20$, $\alpha{=}0.60$).
96	Minimum $T$ for 80\% power at $\alpha{=}0.50$ is approximately $T{=}30$ (extrapolated from simulation; Appendix~\ref{app:power_sim}).
97	The $\alpha{=}0.60$ dip dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), validating $\sqrt{T}$ power scaling; predict-then-verify (commit \texttt{bad72cc}) yields 2/4 boundary predictions within CI (Appendix~\ref{app:t20_prediction_timeline}).
98	
99	
100	\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}
101	\label{sec:generalization}
102	
103	We evaluate PermG across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families, scales 345M--6.9B, and two domains (Table~\ref{tab:generalization}).
104	PermG reaches ${\geq}6/8$ in 11/15 conditions where entropy declines monotonically before diversity; C4 yields strongest detection (Pythia-1.4B: 2/8 WikiText $\to$ 8/8 C4).
105	Non-confirming conditions span precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs fp32: 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8, s43 6/8), and entropy decoupling (SmolLM3-3B: 3/8, entropy 0/4).
106

134	
135	
136	\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}
137	\label{sec:intervention}
138	
139	As an illustrative proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\alpha{=}1.0$, full replacement), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\%$ from generation~0.
140	The 15\% threshold was selected post-hoc (robust band $[10\%, 22\%]$, zero false alarms; Appendix~\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).
141	The predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\% ($56.99$ vs.\ $157.09$; Figure~\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.
142	This single-setting ($\alpha{=}1.0$) demonstration is not rigorously evaluated (CUSUM comparison: Appendix~\ref{app:csmp}).
143	
144	\begin{figure}[t]
145	\centering
146	\includegraphics[width=0.6\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}
147	\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\alpha{=}1.0$). Predictive cessation on entropy (generation~1) reduces peak perplexity by 64\% versus reactive triggering on distinct-1 (generation~2).}
148	\label{fig:intervention}
149	\end{figure}
150

\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\nCross-seed replication shows PermG within ${\\pm}1$, though boundary cells exhibit sensitivity: $[0, 6]/8$ at ($T{=}10$, $\\alpha{=}0.50$); $[0, 3]/8$ at ($T{=}20$, $\\alpha{=}0.60$).\nMinimum $T$ for 80\\% power at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ is approximately $T{=}30$ (extrapolated from simulation; Appendix~\\ref{app:power_sim}).\nThe $\\alpha{=}0.60$ dip dissolves by $T{=}25$ ($5/8$), validating $\\sqrt{T}$ power scaling; predict-then-verify (commit \\texttt{bad72cc}) yields 2/4 boundary predictions within CI (Appendix~\\ref{app:t20_prediction_timeline}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Architecture Validation}\n\\label{sec:generalization}\n\nWe evaluate PermG across 15 model-seed combinations spanning five families, scales 345M--6.9B, and two domains (Table~\\ref{tab:generalization}).\nPermG reaches ${\\geq}6/8$ in 11/15 conditions where entropy declines monotonically before diversity; C4 yields strongest detection (Pythia-1.4B: 2/8 WikiText $\\to$ 8/8 C4).\nNon-confirming conditions span precision sensitivity (GPT-2 bf16: 0/8 vs fp32: 6/8), bidirectional confounding (GPT-2 s43), seed dependence (Pythia-1.4B: s42 2/8, s43 6/8), and entropy decoupling (SmolLM3-3B: 3/8, entropy 0/4).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-architecture PermG forward detection ($\\alpha{=}1.0$, 15 model-seed combinations across five families and two domains).}\n\\label{tab:generalization}\n\\begin{tabular}{lrcl}\n\\toprule\nModel & Params & Domain & PermG fwd \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia-410M    & 410M & Wiki & \\textbf{6/8} \\\\\nPythia-1.4B    & 1.4B & Wiki & 2--6/8$^\\dagger$ \\\\\nPythia-6.9B    & 6.9B & Wiki & \\textbf{7/8}$^*$ \\\\\nGPT-2-medium   & 345M & Wiki & \\textbf{6/8}$^\\S$ \\\\\nOPT-1.3B       & 1.3B & Wiki & \\textbf{6/8} \\\\\nQwen3.5-0.8B   & 0.8B & Wiki & \\textbf{8/8} \\\\\nSmolLM3-3B     & 3.0B & Wiki & 3/8$^\\ddagger$ \\\\\nPadding-410M   & 410M & Wiki & \\textbf{6/8} \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nPythia-1B      & 1.0B & C4   & \\textbf{8/8} \\\\\nPythia-1.4B    & 1.4B & C4   & \\textbf{8/8} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\multicolumn{4}{l}{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$Seed-dep.\\ (s42: 2/8, s43: 6/8); 8-pair incl.\\ MAUVE} \\\\\n\\multicolumn{4}{l}{\\footnotesize \\phantom{$^\\dagger$}(floor ${\\sim}0.033$ at 1.4B; 6-pair in Table~\\ref{tab:14b_phase}). $^*$bf16. $^\\ddagger$ECE-only (ent.\\ 0/4).} \\\\\n\\multicolumn{4}{l}{\\footnotesize $^\\S$s42 fp32: 6/8 fwd, 0 rev; s43: 6/8 both; bf16: 0/8.} \\\\\n\\multicolumn{4}{l}{\\footnotesize 2 seeds each for Pythia-410M/1.4B, GPT-2, OPT, Pythia-1B C4.} \\\\\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Canary-Based Intervention (Proof of Concept)}\n\\label{sec:intervention}\n\nAs an illustrative proof-of-concept under favorable conditions ($\\alpha{=}1.0$, full replacement), we implement predictive cessation: training switches to pure real data when token entropy drops ${>}15\\%$ from generation~0.\nThe 15\\% threshold was selected post-hoc (robust band $[10\\%, 22\\%]$, zero false alarms; Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention_sensitivity}).\nThe predictive trigger fires at generation~1 (one generation before a reactive distinct-1 baseline), reducing peak perplexity by 64\\% ($56.99$ vs.\\ $157.09$; Figure~\\ref{fig:intervention}) in both Pythia-410M and 1.4B.\nThis single-setting ($\\alpha{=}1.0$) demonstration is not rigorously evaluated (CUSUM comparison: Appendix~\\ref{app:csmp}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.6\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_intervention.pdf}\n\\caption{Canary-triggered intervention (Pythia-410M, $\\alpha{=}1.0$). Predictive cessation on entropy (generation~1) reduces peak perplexity by 64\\% versus reactive triggering on distinct-1 (generation~2).}\n\\label{fig:intervention}\n\\end{figure}\n",

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Granger precedence is not causation.}
4	Our claim is scoped to temporal predictive improvement~\cite{granger1969investigating,shojaie2022granger}; bivariate tests cannot exclude confounders, and our cluster-adjusted FPR CI ($[0\%, 41\%]$; \S\ref{sec:limitations}) means true Type~I error may be non-negligible.
5	
6	\paragraph{Non-confirming cells.}
7	Five $\alpha{=}0.60$ cells are excluded from the boundary fit (sync-dip zone: 0/8 at $T{\leq}20$, dissolving to 5/8 at $T{=}25$); the remaining 15/15 match theory (LOO-CV MAE${=}0.037$).
8	The systematic 2/8 gap reflects MAUVE floor saturation ($\mu{=}0.029$).
9	
10	\paragraph{Cross-architecture failure modes.}
11	Four of fifteen conditions do not confirm forward detection (\S\ref{sec:generalization}), spanning GPT-2 precision/confounding, Pythia-1.4B seed dependence, and SmolLM3-3B entropy decoupling (whose non-monotonic entropy violates Proposition~\ref{thm:entropy_leads}'s monotonic-decline assumption, correctly predicting failure).
12	Phase-diagram structure is qualitatively preserved at 1.4B ($\alpha_c$ onset consistent, sync-dip absent, forward detection seed-robust at $T{=}10$); directional asymmetry is seed-sensitive above onset (s43 reverse 6/6 at $\alpha{\geq}0.60$, $T{=}10$; s42: 0/6 across all conditions; Appendix~\ref{app:scale_14b}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Implications.}
15	The ${\geq}1$-generation entropy-diversity lag (14/15 experiments; Proposition~\ref{prop:div_lag}) and PermG directional asymmetry (6/8 forward, 0/8 reverse) support a structural canary mechanism, though directionality is domain-dependent (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}).
16	Our contribution is the phase-diagram methodology, not frontier-scale applicability ($>$7B untested); triggered cessation on entropy reduces peak perplexity by 64\% (\S\ref{sec:intervention}).
17	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01DmmAifBmg2EHU1X4RSAvYX`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Cascade Ordering}
2	\label{sec:cascade}
3	
4	We formalize the empirical regularity that entropy is the earliest available canary metric for diversity collapse.
5	Under mode amplification---the assumption that MLE on peaked distributions concentrates probability toward high-frequency tokens~\cite{shumailov2024curse,dohmatob2024tale}---we derive a formal ordering of metric responses with theoretical grounding (proofs in Appendix~\ref{app:cascade_theory}).
6	
7	\begin{proposition}[Entropy responds before diversity]\label{thm:entropy_leads}
8	Under mode amplification: \textup{(a)} entropy can decrease while the $\epsilon$-effective support $S_\epsilon(p) {=} |\{i: p_i > \epsilon\}|$ (hence distinct-$n$) is unchanged; \textup{(b)} if distinct-$n$ decreases, entropy has already decreased.
9	\end{proposition}
10	
11	The asymmetry arises because entropy responds to any mass redistribution (first-order), while diversity changes only when a token probability crosses the generation threshold~$\epsilon$; the margin $\gamma = \min_{i: p_i > \epsilon}(p_i - \epsilon)$ quantifies how much shift diversity absorbs silently.
12	
13	\begin{proposition}[Diversity lag $\geq 1$ generation]\label{prop:div_lag}
14	If the per-generation perturbation satisfies $\alpha\|\mathcal{T}(p_t) - p_t\|_\infty < \gamma(p_t, \epsilon)$ through entropy onset $t_H$, then diversity onset $t_D \geq t_H + 1$.
15	\end{proposition}
16	
17	ECE co-moves with entropy at zero lag: peakier conditionals simultaneously decrease per-token entropy and increase confidence ($\Delta\mathrm{ECE} \approx \Delta C > 0$ whenever $\Delta\bar{H} < 0$; qualitative argument, Appendix~\ref{app:cascade_theory}).
18	The predicted cascade $\{H, \mathrm{ECE}\} \to \mathrm{Distinct}$-$n$ is confirmed in 14/15 experiments at $\alpha \geq 0.30$ (GPT-2-medium s43 bidirectional) (within-domain WikiText, $2\sigma$ onset threshold): entropy and ECE onset at generation~1, distinct-$n$ at generation~2 ($t_H{=}t_{\mathrm{ECE}}{=}1$, $t_D{=}2$ uniformly; \S\ref{sec:regime}; Appendix~\ref{app:moment_cascade}).
19	Five low-$\alpha$ experiments show no detectable onset, leaving the ordering undefined below the detection threshold.
20	Assumption~4 (mode amplification) holds when the model progressively concentrates probability mass on high-frequency tokens; SmolLM3-3B (entropy 0/4; Table~\ref{tab:generalization}) provides an empirical counter-example where this assumption does not hold, possibly due to architectural differences in attention or normalization.
21	The cascade establishes entropy as the \emph{optimal canary metric}---the earliest available signal---and explains PermG's directional selectivity: forward tests detect temporal precedence (6/8) while reverse tests find none (0/8) at $\alpha \geq 0.50$.
22	
```

</details>

现在我已通读全文。开始系统性叙事审查。先记录问题清单，然后逐节修改。

**全文叙事问题清单：**

**Abstract** (abstract.tex)
- [major] 开头太弱——"proceeds silently until collapse is advanced" 缺乏紧迫感，没有用同行论文常用的"数据危机+具体数字"hook
- [major] 中间段过度堆砌技术细节（"13 contamination levels, five families, 345M--6.9B"），应精简到 story 推进所需
- [minor] 最后一句 "proof-of-concept intervention" 收尾平淡

**Introduction** (introduction.tex)
- [critical] P3 读起来像实验报告："We systematically map this landscape using Pythia-410M across 13 contamination levels..."——这是 Method 该说的
- [major] P2→P3 缺乏过渡，从"question"直接跳到"what we did"
- [major] 贡献列表前缺少一段总结性发现（what we find），让读者先理解结论再看具体贡献
- [minor] 贡献3重复了前文已经说过的 FPR 范围

**Cascade Ordering** (theory.tex)
- [major] 开头第一句读起来像实验报告（"We formalize the empirical regularity..."）
- [minor] 最后一句"optimal canary metric"和"explains PermG's directional selectivity"混在一起，缺层次

**Method** (method.tex)
- [critical] §3.2 PermG 段 (L23-29) 是统计数字的墙，几乎不可读——需要拆分并让核心 insight 突出
- [major] Method Sensitivity Analysis 段落过密，多处统计细节可移至 appendix 引用
- [major] "Shape comparison with smooth alternatives" 读起来像分析日志

**Experiments** (experiments.tex)
- [critical] Sharp Detection Onset 段 (L16-39) 严重超载——24行连续段落，夹杂大量括号注和附录引用
- [major] Cross-Architecture Validation (L100-133) 是表格的文字复述，缺乏洞察
- [major] Canary scoping 段落（L31-39）一个段落承载了5个独立论点
- [minor] Dose-response 段落结构合理

**Discussion** (discussion.tex)
- [critical] 过于简短和条列化——像要点列表改写的段落，缺乏综合性讨论
- [major] "Implications" 段只有2句，对于一篇有4个 claims 的论文来说严重不足
- [major] 没有讨论 findings 对领域的意义和未来方向

**全文通用问题：**

  "new_string": "\\begin{abstract}\nWhen language models train on their own output, distributional quality degrades well before standard metrics register collapse.\nDetecting this degradation early is difficult: the time series are short ($T{\\leq}25$ generations), the signal is sub-linear in contamination fraction, and, as we show, the choice of statistical test can shift conclusions from universal detection to complete null.\nWe map the detectability landscape of iterative model collapse, primarily on Pythia-410M across 13 contamination levels with cross-architecture validation on five families (345M--6.9B).\nUsing permutation Granger causality\\footnote{Granger precedence establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causation~\\cite{shojaie2022granger}.} (PermG), we identify a detection boundary $\\alpha_c(T)$ that follows a power law in observation length (leave-one-$T$-out CV: MAE${=}0.037$, 15/15 cells within CI), structured into four regimes: a blind zone, a sharp onset, a non-monotonic dip, and a reliable detection region.\nFive statistical methods applied to identical data yield null-control false positive rates from 3\\% to 92\\%, exposing method sensitivity as a primary concern rather than a robustness footnote.\nEntropy Granger-precedes diversity collapse by ${\\geq}1$ generation (14/15 experiments), providing a canary signal whose triggered cessation reduces peak perplexity by 64\\%.\n\\end{abstract}"
